### MOST INGENIOUS

->Or<-

# **Of the Great**
->And<-
# **Little Farmer.**

Opened and wide-standing chest
of the greatest and most skillful
**secrets of nature /**

quite clearly through a

**Warning Instruction and Proof**
against all those / who falsely persuade
others that *Drinkable Gold* <term>Aurum potabile; a legendary medicinal elixir thought to grant longevity</term>

outside of the tincture of the universal *Philosopher’s Stone* <term>Lapidis Philosophici; the ultimate goal of alchemy, believed to enable the transmutation of metals and the healing of all illnesses</term>.

# F V. C J

->Hermes.<-

*In the upper sphere, in the middle of the source, is a vein, which is the first Rule of the Philosophers.* <note>original Latin: "In superiori Sphæra est in medio fontis vena, quæ est Philosophorum Regula prima." In alchemical symbolism, the "source" or "spring" often refers to the primordial matter or "mercury" from which all things flow.</note>

---

->Arros.<-

*All metals are of the same substance in terms of purity: they differ only in impurity, and through greater or lesser digestion.* <note>original Latin: "Metalla omnia quoad puritatem sunt ejusdem substantiæ: differunt tantum impuritate, majori vel minori digestione."</note> <term>Digestion; in alchemy, this refers to the process of exposing a substance to steady, gentle heat to mature or "ripen" it, mimicking the natural growth of metals within the earth.</term>

## To the Christian Reader
### God’s Grace be with you first.

Although I have never occupied myself with hands-on labor in **alchemical** <term>Alchemical: Referring here to the "Art" of chemical transformation and the study of the hidden properties of matter.</term> matters—since, as you should know, such things are not my **profession** <note>The author distinguishes himself here as a scholar or "theoreticus" rather than a "laborant" (one who works at the furnace), which was a common distinction in 17th-century scientific writing.</note>—I nevertheless find no small pleasure and delight in achieving a true **understanding** of the genuine **powers** within the **creations** of **Almighty** GOD. For, as I also at times from

<summary>In this preface, the author introduces himself to the reader, clarifying that while he is not a practicing alchemist by trade, he studies the subject to better understand the divine powers hidden within nature. He frames alchemical knowledge as a way to appreciate the craftsmanship of the Creator.</summary>

<keywords>Christian reader, alchemical theory, divine creation, natural philosophy, 17th-century preface, study of nature</keywords>

# The Opened Casket
## of the Greatest Mystery
## of Nature.

### First, the Great Peasant.

<note>The "Great Peasant" (German: *Der Grosse Baur*) likely refers to the work *Aureum Saeculum Redivivum* by Adrian von Mynsicht, often attributed to a "Great Peasant" figure in alchemical circles, emphasizing that true wisdom often comes from humble sources or the "work of the hands."</note>

->The growth comes from Him who is the Beginning and the End.<-

The holy Apostle James 
says in Chapter 1: Every good gift 
and every perfect gift comes 
from above, from the Father of 
Lights, with whom there is no variation nor 
change between light and darkness. Paul 
speaks in 1 Corinthians 3: I have planted, Apollos has 
watered, but God gives the growth.

Alanus the Philosopher <note>original: "Alanus Phil."; likely referring to Alan of Lille (Alanus de Insulis), a 12th-century scholar whose name was frequently invoked in alchemical texts.</note>: "Son, set your heart more toward 
God than toward the <term>Art</term> <note>The "Art" (German: *Kunst*) refers to alchemy, specifically the "Great Work" of purifying and perfecting matter.</note>, for it is a gift from 
God, and He shares it with whom He wills; there-
fore, have peace and joy in God, and so you shall possess 
the Art." Alphidius <note>An early, possibly Arabic, alchemical authority often cited in the *Rosarium Philosophorum*.</note>: "Son, you should know that 
you cannot attain this Art unless you 
have purified your heart and mind toward God, 
and reconciled yourself heartily with Him."

<meta>catchword: when</meta>

<summary>This opening page of the treatise establishes a spiritual foundation for alchemy, framing the "Art" as a divine gift. It quotes the Apostles James and Paul, as well as legendary alchemists Alanus and Alphidius, to emphasize that success in the work requires a purified heart and devotion to God.</summary>

<keywords>The Opened Casket, Great Peasant, Art, alchemy, spirituality, Alanus, Alphidius, divine gift, purification</keywords>

After that, the Stone of the Moon, in which the pure and clear, incombustible white Sulfur dominates, just as the *The Sound of the Trumpet* <note>original: *Clangor Buccinæ*</note> in the *Assembly of Philosophers* <note>original: *Turba [Philosophorum]*</note> on page 484 explains this. In the Lunar subject there is a white Sulfur, and the Stone in which our <term>Mercury: the alchemical principle of spirit and fluidity; original "Mercurius"</term> resides holds within itself both natures, both the white and the red. And this is the basis of the entire mastery, for our Mercury is the earth; whatever one sows therein, that it brings forth. Finally, there is the third stone, which is a medium between the two and encloses both of their natures within itself; *For the Stone of Mercury embraces both natures* <note>original: *Nam Lapis Mercurii amplectitur utramq; naturam*</note>, as has been said. ### The Great Peasant

...metals are born. In this matter, let no one be misled by the fact that they <note>Referring to various alchemical authorities.</note> speak only of two—namely <term>Sulfur: the alchemical principle of "soul" and combustibility; original: *Sulphure*</term> and <term>Mercury: the alchemical principle of "spirit" and fluidity; original: *Mercurio*</term>—and leave out the <term>Salt: the alchemical principle of "body" and stability; original: *Sal*</term>. But you should be informed <margin><note>symbol for Mercury</note></margin> that the Salt is hidden within the Mercurial water, and that water can very soon and easily be transformed into the <term>Salt of metals: a crystallized metallic base; original: *Sal metallorum*</term>; likewise, Salt can become water again.

In addition, mention has also been made that the metals are born from a salty, <term>vitriolic: related to sulfates or "vitriol," which were seen as acidic, life-giving salts; original: *victriolisch*</term> water and a sulfurous vapor.

*The Path of Paths* <note>original: *Semita semitæ*; a common title for alchemical tracts, often attributed to Albertus Magnus.</note> in the *Assembly of Philosophers* <note>original: *Turba*; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum*, one of the most ancient and influential Latin alchemical texts.</note> on page 473 states: You should understand and note that the Mercury which is thus cooked—the son of all metals—is <term>digested: a process of slow, steady maturation through heat; original: *digerirt*</term> imperfectly in the belly of the earth through sulfurous heat or vapors. According to the distinction of this "sulfurousness"—that is, depending on whether the sulfurous vapor is pure or impure—different metals are born in the earth. However, their initial <term>matter: the "prima materia" or first substance; original: *materia*</term> is of one kind; they differ only in that one is cooked more, or more "burnt" by impure sulfurous vapors than the other, and thus they are born differently. In this, all <term>Philosophers: a common term for alchemists and natural scientists; original: *Philosophi*</term> agree.

It would be far too long and extensive to set down here <term>word-for-word: original: *verbotenus*</term> the evidence of all the philosophers, since they all agree with one another; you may read <note>Bernhard Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist known for his search for the Philosopher's Stone.</note> Count Bernhard himself in the cited places. Also see the *Assembly* <note>original: *Turbam*</note> on pages 495, 356, and 476.

# Opened Cabinet of the Nature

The *Dialogue of Philosophy* <note>original: *Dialogus Philosophiæ*</note>, which is explained on pages 14 and 16, states that one must not take that from which the **metals** <note>original: *metalla*</note> originated. No: rather, one must take that which is extracted from the **metals**, and it is precisely that which lies hidden within the **metals**. This is explained quite elegantly in the **Turba** <note>The *Turba Philosophorum* (Assembly of the Philosophers), one of the earliest and most influential Latin alchemical texts, written as a debate between ancient sages.</note>, part 1, page 577, where it provides the example of a tree. When one wishes to produce a tree, one must not take water or the earth, but rather something that comes from the tree itself, such as the seed or a small graft; this is then entrusted back to Nature. You can read of this again in **Ripley** <note>George Ripley (c. 1415–1490), a famous English alchemist and canon of Bridlington.</note> in his *Axioms*, page 179. 
<margin>☞</margin> The composite metallic mass is **lead-glance** <note>original: *Bleyschweiff*, a historical term for galena or lead ore.</note>; therefore we rightly call it **lead**. The properties of its luster come from the **Sun** <note>original: *Sole*, representing Gold.</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: *Luna*, representing Silver.</note>.

**Hermes, Great King and Father of Philosophers**, says in his book *On General Chaos* <note>original: *de Chao generali*</note>, Chapter 19, page 268, number 14: 
<margin>☞</margin> The most noble purification of our **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii*</note> is to strip away its leaden darkness or outward form through the **aid of wine** <note>Likely referring to "spirit of wine" (rectified ethanol), used as a solvent to extract the "essences" of minerals.</note>, so that it may be made magnificent, clear, and translucent, like crystalline transparent salt. For it is undeniable: the **metallic form** <note>original: *forma metallica*</note> must be removed so that a **spiritual essence** may be created, as will be mentioned in the section on **Solution** <note>The alchemical stage where a solid is dissolved into a liquid state, often described as a "death" of the metal before its "resurrection."</note>. **Dionysius Zachaire** <note>A 16th-century French alchemist known for his accounts of the high costs and dedication required for the Great Work.</note>, page 92, says that we should and must take the very same **matter** <note>original: *materiam*</note> from which Nature in the earth [creates] the...

# The Great Peasant.

Count Bernhard <note>Bernhard Trevisan (1406–1490), a celebrated Italian alchemist who spent his life and fortune seeking the Philosopher's Stone.</note> explains that metal is made because Nature first creates a **leaden matter** <note>original: *bleyechte Materiam*</note>.

to unlock, as **Flamel** <note>Nicholas Flamel, the 14th-century French notary and legendary alchemist.</note> testifies on folios 153 and 154, where he asks: why do we not take the pure bodies **of the Sun and Moon** <note>original: *Solis & Lunæ*, referring to gold and silver</note> for our work? 
Answer: because Nature has knotted them together so tightly that one cannot easily affect them with fire. Instead, we take a **Body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> in which there is just such a pure **Sulfur** and **Mercury** as there is in silver or gold, but in 
which Nature has only worked a little and has left it imperfect. He further says that we should 
<margin>☞</margin> take one that is not "complicated" <note>likely meaning "not complexly bound" or "not fully fixed"</note>, for the **Sun** and **Moon** are perfect; therefore, they are already at their highest grade. One may read further there, 
where he quite elegantly explains the **manner of the materials** <note>original: *modum materiarum*</note>. It is also well to note here that the **Philosophers** <note>alchemical authors</note> always use the plural, such as "bodies," **metals, of metals, from metals** <note>original: *metalla, metallis, metallorum*</note>; they do not say "the Body" or

as proven above, stem from a single root, and

the **Universal** <note>The Universal Medicine or Elixir</note> can be prepared from them specifically 
<margin>☞</margin> before they enter the fire, and especially while they 
still remain in their **ore** <note>original: *minera*</note>. Therefore, **Roger Bacon** <note>A 13th-century English friar and polymath often credited with alchemical treatises.</note> and **Flamel** (folio 137) say that nothing clings to the metals...

### The Great Peasant

...selected / and he / who reads these **proofs** <note>original: "probationes"</note> and **authorities** <note>original: "autoritates"</note> / will not be able to deny / that the same and no other is the right, true **Matter of the Stone** <note>original: "Materia des Lapidis"; the primary substance required to create the Philosopher's Stone</note>. Since all metals come from and grow out of it.

Now we want to come closer / and speak of the very nearest matter: For there have been several **Philosophers** / who out of a whole **pound** <note>represented by the abbreviation "lb."</note> have barely obtained half a **Loth** <note>A historical German unit of weight, roughly 1/32nd of a pound or 15 grams. The author is highlighting how little "true" essence is found in common matter</note> / that was suitable for the Work / as *Clangor* <note>referring to the alchemical text *Clangor Buccinae* or "The Clanging of the Trumpet"</note> testifies on **folio** 478. And in this is a great advantage / because nature has placed more **Mercury** or **saltpeter** <note>original: "Salis nitri"</note> in one / and has also cooked the one **matter** more or longer / than the other / therefore it is well to consider / that more is contained in those / where nature has worked the most / but yet has not stepped beyond its **limit** <note>original: "Termin"; the point at which nature’s natural development stops and the alchemist’s art must begin</note> / otherwise it would be too difficult to dissolve / of this says **Flamel** <note>Nicolas Flamel (c. 1330–1418), a famous French scrivener and legendary alchemist</note> folio 152. and 153. This is regarding the most hidden thing / out of which internal thing it can or must next be taken / this explains **Arnold of Villanova** <note>A major 13th-century physician and alchemist</note> in his *Rosary* <note>original: *Rosarium Philosophorum* (The Rose Garden of the Philosophers), a highly influential series of alchemical woodcuts and texts</note> folio 404. There are several mediocre matters / among which one is more purified by nature than the others / also more and longer cooked and **digested** <note>original: "digerirt"; refers to *digestion*, the process of applying constant, gentle heat to matter to mature it, mimicking the heat of the earth's core</note>, and these same are better and nearest to the Work. # The Opened Chest of Nature

...send and name the places / how one may obtain them / and I expect I shall thus satisfy that person / if not do too much.

I admonish you, however, dear brother in Christ / you who receive this my instruction / that you should fear God's judgment / for you must give an account of any abuse / and you should keep such high secrets silent / they are **secrets** <note>original Latin: *arcana*</note>, and **chest** <note>original Latin: *arca*</note> means a box / therein one should keep them / preserve them and hide them from false people / when that happens / then the dear God will also give His blessing to it / and there is no doubt / if the gentleman is of a good intention / his desire will succeed well / for the other points will be written just as clearly. Regarding secrecy, I have no doubt / otherwise God is not in the game / and no true faith and loyalty would be present. You have sufficiently understood from the **cited passages** <note>original Latin: *allegatis locis*</note> of the **Philosophers'** <note>original Latin: *Philosophorum*</note> books / that they call the **matter** <note>original Latin: *materiam*</note> **lead** <note>original Latin: *plumbum*</note>, **graphite** <note>original Latin: *plumbaginem*; referring to various lead-bearing ores like galena</note>, **litharge** <note>original Latin: *Lytargyrum*; lead monoxide, often a byproduct of silver refining</note>, **lead of copper** <note>original Latin: *plumbum æris*</note>, and all these things agree / although one might have had a **nearer matter** <note>original: *nähere materiam*; a substance already partially refined by nature</note> than the other / yet it can be made just as well from the **more remote** <note>original Latin: *remotiore*</note> or most distant / but with greater toil and labor: one contains more **mercury** <note>original Latin: *Mercurii*</note> or **metallic salt** <note>original Latin: *salis metallici*</note>...

He also identifies the various names given to the primary alchemical matter, such as lead, litharge, and graphite, noting that while some sources are easier to work with than others, all can eventually yield the desired result.</summary>

### The Great Peasant
E
<meta>catchword: stim-</meta>

Thus you have, my dear friend, the instruction not only regarding the harmonies <note>original: "Concordanten"; referring to the points of agreement between various alchemical authors.</note> of the philosophers, but also of the materials. May the eternal Almighty God grant that you may use it to His honor and for the benefit of your neighbor.

How one should proceed further will follow in the second part; this treasure is not to be paid for with any gold, as Solomon says in his Wisdom in the 7th chapter, and silver is to be reckoned like common sand against it. <note>A reference to Wisdom of Solomon 7:9: "Neither compared I unto her any precious stone, because all gold in respect of her is as a little sand, and silver shall be counted as clay before her."</note>

Other things will also otherwise be shown concerning the **GUR** <term>GUR; in early modern mining and alchemy, this was believed to be a "metallic milk" or primordial vapor in the earth that had not yet hardened into solid ore.</term> and the *first matter* <note>original: "prima materia"; the formless primeval substance from which all elements were created.</note>, and there is no doubt at all, for I might well have seen and experienced enough that I could—if it were possible—prove it with deeds; but it cannot be. Thus you have, my dear friend, the entire *treatise* <note>original: "Tractatum"</note> completely regarding the whole of the materials, in which all philosophers agree.

It might well have been somewhat shorter, but because I did not know whether the *heir* <note>original: "hæres"; likely referring to the intended recipient or student of this manuscript.</note> knows Latin or not, I have set it down twice, though with difficulty; my son, simply look up the books of the philosophers, you will not find it otherwise, even though I can now prove the *entire operation* <note>original: "totam operationem"</note> with deeds, praise be to God; and in this point the best thing is that one [finds] the philosophers *agreeing* <note>original: "concordantes"</note> with one another and as one—

<summary>The author concludes the first part of his treatise, emphasizing that his instructions align with the hidden wisdom of the "philosophers" and the biblical praise of wisdom over gold. He notes that he has provided explanations in both German and Latin to ensure his "heir" or student understands the material.</summary>
<keywords>harmonies, Solomon, GUR, first matter, treatise, inheritance, chemical operation, concordance</keywords>

<note>original: "LILIUM INTER SPINAS"; a common biblical and alchemical metaphor representing the precious truth found amidst difficulties or the "purity" of the work hidden within "thorny" matter.</note>

I preferred her before scepters and thrones, and riches—if they be compared with her—I count as nothing; nor do I consider precious gems to be comparable to her.

Likewise, all **gold** <note>original symbol: ☉ (the Sun/Sol, representing gold)</note> compared with her is like mere sand, and before her, **silver** <note>original symbol: ☽ (the Moon/Luna, representing silver)</note> shall be counted as common clay. I loved her more than health and beauty, and I chose to have her instead of light, for her brilliance is never extinguished.

But along with her, all good things came to me at once...

# The Little Peasant.

<term>foundation <note>original: "fundament"; the catchword "amdent" from page 76 completes this word.</note></term> if you wish to know it, do not let it trouble you to read of it. For <note>original: "Nam 'qui vult sentire commodum, oportet ut etiam sentiat onus"</note> **he who wishes to feel the benefit must also feel the burden**, it would be far too long for me to recount them all to you; and when I have now fully instructed you, you shall not see or speak with me again until you have brought the <term>Work <note>The "Great Work" (Magnum Opus) of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note></term> to its end. 
<margin><image-desc>Manicule pointing left</image-desc></margin>
Then I will speak much more kindly with you, and at that same time you will also know me better, love me more fervently, and hold me in higher honor than you do now; and thereafter we shall not soon be parted; let that be sufficient of this matter.

Furthermore, you should be informed that he who understands the origin of metals will also well know that the <term>Matter <note>original: "Materi"</note></term> of our <term>Stone</term> 
<margin><image-desc>Manicule pointing left</image-desc></margin>
must also be metallic; yet it is no metal, nor any *mineral*, but rather *metals* and *minerals* and *minerals* and *metals*. For the kind and nature of them all is in one thing, which is called <term>unripe mineral electrum <note>original: "Electrum minerale immeturum"; likely a misspelling of "immaturum". This refers to a raw, mixed metallic substance before it has been refined by nature into a specific metal.</note></term>, **Magnesia**, 
<margin><image-desc>Manicule pointing left</image-desc></margin>
or **Lunaria** <note>These are allegorical names for the "First Matter". Magnesia was a term for the universal transformative substance, while Lunaria (moon-plant) suggests a silver-like or cold, watery nature.</note>. Therefore the **Philosophers** always use the <term>plural <note>original: "pluralitatem"</note></term>, such as *metals*, *of metals*, *with metals*, etc. I must not speak more clearly, nor is it necessary, because the *matter* is already known to you; otherwise others might hear of it, for many <term>tempters <note>original: "Versucher"; this can mean "experimenters" or "seekers," but in this context, it refers to those who search for the secret with unworthy or greedy intentions.</note></term> lurk about this mountain; some come quite close, and some even climb up, but I do not let myself [be seen] by them along with the

But now they are ashamed of this: that they, as degreed persons <note>Graduated academics who hold formal university titles.</note>, are still expected to learn. Therefore 
the *true philosophy* <note>original: *vera Philosophia*; referring to Hermetic or alchemical wisdom based on the observation of nature rather than academic textbooks.</note> must be innocently condemned under the appearance of the false, persecuted and blasphemed to the utmost; but so it must go and happen, that God’s wisdom is called foolishness before the world, and vice versa. For

*God, not without a weighty judgment, willed wisdom to be revealed under the name of foolishness, so that the mystery of His power would truly remain a secret; but in the end, our good cause shall triumph.* <note>original Latin: *Deus non ſine gravi judicio ſapientiam ſub nomine ſtultitiæ voluit eſſe revelatam ut nimirum myſterium virtutis ſuæ eſſet arcanum ; ſed tandem bona cauſa noſtra triumphabit.*</note>

Furthermore, I would very much like to know what the **nearer matter** <term>propinquior materia: the specific starting substance chosen for the work, as opposed to the 'remote' primary matter of all things</term> of the Stone might be, from which I should or could extract the **specific form** <term>formam specificam: the essential quality or "soul" of the substance</term> or the **two flowers** <note>In alchemy, the "two flowers" typically refer to the White Elixir (which transforms metals into silver) and the Red Elixir (which transforms them into gold).</note>. ☞ In that case, you are mixing two questions together for me, though you only asked for one; however, I can tell you this much: that you should carefully consider the four seasons of the year, and thereafter divide the Work accordingly; the books of the wise <note>Refers to the "Philosophers" or established alchemical authors</note> treat this subject sufficiently. Children: Thus it is to be understood of all creatures <note>In this context, "creatures" refers to all created things in the natural world—plants, animals, and minerals—not just living animals.</note>: For the earth has been a mother of all four-footed animals, herbs, trees, foliage, and grass; and yet it was initially one single thing, namely the earth; and then, in and with the earth, the seed. # Nature's Opened Chest / On the Generation of Man and Animals.

Regarding humans and all kinds of animals that possess a sentient life, however, there is a different view: when the mingling of man and woman occurs, the <term>Astra</term> <note>original: "Astra." In the tradition of Paracelsian alchemy, these are the "inner stars" or spiritual forces within a living being that correspond to the celestial bodies.</note> rise up within the animal or human; these are so strong that they overcome the stars, and the stars in turn overcome the eternal light (for every <term>matter</term> <note>original: "Materia."</note> draws its form toward itself). Thus, the projection of the constellations occurs simultaneously with the seed into the <term>womb</term> <note>original: "Matricem."</note>. From this comes the movement, so that the male <term>seed</term> <note>original: "Sperma."</note>, acting as the active principle, mingles with the female seed, works within it, and creates a human or animal according to the shape and form that the stars and the firmament possessed at that very moment. When this <term>body</term> <note>original: "Corpus."</note> is ready, the second projection occurs—namely, the <term>astral</term>, material, spiritual, and <term>firmamental</term> body, along with life itself.

Just as the stars stood at that very moment, so the human and the animal reveal themselves in their senses and thoughts. From this, the difference between humans and animals is recognized: that some may be human in outward shape, but possess a dog-like, wolf-like, or bear-like dis-

<meta>The page ends mid-word with the prefix "Ge-", which is completed by the catchword "müth" to form "Gemüth," meaning "disposition" or "mind."</meta>

### Nature’s Cabinet Opened

I asked: Will this **bloom** be called the **first entity** <note>original: *primum Ens*. An alchemical term referring to the primordial essence or the fundamental state of a substance before it becomes dense matter.</note> about which **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), a pioneering Swiss physician and alchemist who emphasized the spiritual properties of minerals.</note> wrote so many wonders?

He answered: Yes, but it is incorrectly named and understood. For **marcasites**, **cobalt spirits** <note>original: *Cobolden*. In early mining and alchemy, "cobolds" or "kobolds" were both the spirits of the mines and the ores—like cobalt—that were seen as "mocking" miners because they yielded no silver when smelted.</note>, etc., are not the **first entities**, nor is it that which **Theophrastus** teaches to draw out through **sublimation** or **distillation**. Rather, it is the very beginning of the **seed**, or the **ultimate spiritual entity of metals or minerals** <note>original: *Ultimum Ens Spirituale Metallorum vel Mineralium*</note>; thus it may be titled.

However, within it lies hidden the **first entity**, which is the **celestial ethereal fire**. This fire nonetheless carries its own subject with it and is united with it. You must separate these from one another—not merely through **sublimation**, whereby you only obtain the **bloom** or flowers of the metals and minerals, plucking them from their stalks, as it were, and harvesting them from the weeds—that is, from the mountains and other impure things. Instead, you must first draw the **first entity** out of these materials, in which it lies more lightly and is much easier to obtain than from its **metal**. For in this state, it is still soft, tender, mild, and spread apart—volatile and pure. In the **metal**, it is locked together, hard, coarse, and **fixed** <term>fixed</term> <note>In alchemy, a "fixed" substance is stable and resists the action of fire, whereas a "volatile" substance evaporates or transforms easily.</note>. 

In the same way (as said before), you can bring out the taste and lovely scent from **roses** and **lavender** through **distillation** as a simpler path; for you also

Therefore, no fixed period of time can be set for such things, though as far as gold is concerned, its life span may last for several ages.

### Chymical Kabbalah

Secondly, regarding your belief that because no metal in the earth is transformed into another, it must follow that such transformation is impossible through Art. However, a **digestion** <term>digestion: a process of slow, gentle heating to mature a substance</term> must also occur.

...matter; therefore, because there are many kinds of forms, it follows that there must also be many kinds of matter. And just as the form, being pure, removes the impurities <term>excrementa: waste products or dross separated from the essence during the work</term> from the impure matter and cleanses it, and only then performs a proper mixing, from which a fruit follows according to the nature of the form—so too does Art <note>Art: in this context, the "Great Art" of alchemy</note> perform the same. When one prepares <note>original: *præpari-rirt*</note> a pure form and casts it upon impure metals, such a form separates the impure form and matter from that matter and impure metal. Once this has occurred, a proper union <term>conjunction: the alchemical marriage or joining of two substances into one</term> follows, and a pure metal is found.

But if the form is prepared to such a high and subtle degree, as I have taught you to do, and is then multiplied through itself <note>multiplication: a process in the Great Work where the power and quantity of the alchemical "medicine" is increased</note>, it becomes so full of virtue and power that it does not merely cast off and eject the mineral dross from either the form or the matter of the imperfect metals; rather, it tinctures <term>tinctures: the process of the Elixir staining or transforming the inner nature of a base metal into gold or silver</term> them all in the best possible way <note>original: *m. e. a.*, likely an abbreviation for *modo et arte*, meaning "by way and art"</note>, renewing and rejuvenating them into the highest and best metal. 

Conversely, if one makes such a multiplied form from the lowest metal—namely, from **Jupiter** <term>Jove: the alchemical name for tin</term> or **Saturn** <term>Saturno: the alchemical name for lead</term>—the imperfection and dross <term>fæculentia: sediment or impurities</term> multiply within it to such an extent and become so powerful that if such a form were cast upon a perfect body <term>corpus: the physical substance of a metal</term> or metal...

# The Unlocked Chest of Nature

the **first matter** <term>prima materia: the raw, unrefined base substance used at the start of the alchemical Great Work</term>, but rather the threefold **extraction** <note>original: "extractio"</note> and **Essence** <note>original: "Essentia"</note> from the **first matter of gemstones** <note>original: "prima materia Gemmarum"</note>. # Chemical Cabala

...if you follow this and perform the second and the third **separation** <note>original: "separation"; the process of dividing a substance into its elemental or spiritual components</note>, then there is no longer a carbuncle <note>A legendary red gemstone, often representing the most concentrated mineral essence or the Philosopher's Stone itself</note>, for that would be as lead compared to gold; rather, it is a small heavenly fire, pure, delicate, and clear. # The Opened Chest of Nature #

The reason for this regeneration, however, is because **God** the Lord—on account of the terrible Fall of *Adam* <note>original Latin: "Adæ"</note>—cursed the Earth (that is, the elemental, corporeal, and lowest part of the world) and subjected it to *corruption* <note>original: "Corruption"</note>. Consequently, neither the *plants* <note>original: "Vegetabilia"</note> nor the *minerals* <note>original: "Mineralia"</note>, nor even the *animals* <note>original: "Animalia"</note>, can regain their first happy state or their powerful nature without regeneration; much less can they reach their lost perfection.

And since *Philosophical Regeneration* <note>original: "Regeneratio Philosophica"</note> is nothing other than a *purification* <note>original: "Purgatio"</note> and separation of the good from the evil—and furthermore, since the *soul* <note>original: "Anima"</note> is the highest thing within it, which is life; followed by the *spirit* <note>original: "Spiritus"</note>; and finally the *body* <note>original: "Corpus"</note>, which is dead compared to the other two—the *soul*, as well as the *spirit* and *body*, must first be *separated* <note>original: "separiret"</note> from their corruptible nature. Thus, subsequently, these three in the *philosophical work* <note>original: "opere Philosophico"</note> become pure *soul* or *quintessences* <term>quintæ essentiæ|The "fifth essence" or purest state of a substance, beyond the four physical elements</term>. For if one gives a *body*—whatever kind one wishes—to the regenerated *soul* (though understand that the *body* must have a *symbolic affinity* <term>symbolum|A shared nature or correspondence between substances that allows them to unite</term> with that same *soul*, otherwise *anything could come from anything* <note>original: "ex quolibet quidlibet"</note>), then the *body* must follow the *soul*, and not the *soul* follow the *body*. The reason: life is in the *soul*, and even more so in the regenerated soul, which is an everlasting life; but the old *body* by itself is...

In short, the entire **Work of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Opus Philosophorum"</note> is nothing other than making new heavens and earths: pulling the heaven down and swinging the earth above itself, raising and placing it on high in the place of heaven, and proceeding in the same way as God the Lord acted in the creation of the world. 

For in the beginning, when God the Lord created heaven and earth, it was all only one water, which was the **first matter** <note>original: "Prima Materia"</note>. In this, heaven and earth with their whole host—along with the abyss of the earth, encompassed with darkness, empty, without power or life—were submerged and contained. Because of this, perhaps, **Democritus** was moved to establish his **atoms** <note>original: "Atomos"</note>—as a watery vapor, smoke, or dissolved water—as the **first principles of things** <note>original: "pro principiis rerum"</note>. # The Opened Cabinet of Nature #

...the uppermost part <note>original: "obersten Theil"</note>, the Spirit of God—who is an unconsuming fire and life itself—hovered and preserved that same part. But the abyss of the earth and the darkness—as the most useless, dead part—He abandoned; He drew only the powers of light and life out of the earth into the water, so that right at the very beginning, a **separation** <term>separation|A fundamental alchemical process of dividing substances into their pure and impure components</term> occurred through the Spirit of God within this **solution** <term>solution|The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state, often seen as the first step in purification</term> and **putrefaction** <term>putrefaction|The breakdown or 'death' of a substance, which alchemists believed was necessary for its rebirth into a higher form</term>.

Therefore, the Spirit of God embraced only the water as the uppermost, most powerful, and best part, and enclosed it with His **almighty power** <note>original: "Allmächtigen Krafft"</note>, while leaving the weak and dead matter lying in the abyss of **darkness** <note>original: "Finsternüß"</note>. Since, then, the essence and life of all creatures were contained within this solution, the Spirit of God rightly nurtured, covered, and strengthened it—much like a hen with her chicks <note>This "brooding" or "incubation" imagery refers to the divine heat required to bring life out of the chaotic primal matter.</note>—under the wings of His almighty fertility, ripening it for the increase of His perfect light of life. 

Now, when everything in this solution and putrefaction had been ripened by the Spirit of God and divided into its perfect, active state of life, the true essential **separation** followed: namely, that God, through the command of His almighty Word, first extracted from this solution the most clarified, subtlest, most powerful, and highly purified part—indeed, all the **crystalline clarity** <note>original: "Crystallinische Klarheit"</note> and radiance...

### Chemical Cabala ###

...be called from *Fire* and *Water* or *Heavens* <note>original: *Isch* and *Majim* or *Schamajim*; the author is using Hebrew etymology where *Shamayim* (heavens) is seen as a combination of *Esh* (fire) and *Mayim* (water)</note>.

The middle water has been divided through separation <note>original: *Separation*</note> into the visible heavens / such as into the planetary orbits <note>original: *orbes Planetarum*</note>, into the Firmament <note>original: *Firmamentum*</note> or the "First Moved" <note>original: *primum mobile*; in the old model of the universe, this was the outermost sphere that moved all the others</note> / from each of which their essence <note>original: *Essentia*</note>, that is the seven planets / along with other innumerable stars <note>original: *Syderibus*</note> were first drawn out and separated. And thereafter they were placed back into them / just as some are united and bound with their orbits <note>original: *orbibus*</note> and their own proper motions <note>original: *propriis moribus*</note>. These Middle-Waters or Orbs of the Stars <note>original: *Orbes Syderum*</note> are of a moderate nature along with their stars / having the quality of both the Soul and the Spirit / hidden within them according to the manner of the Spirit <note>original: *Spiritus*</note>: Therefore the uppermost and lowermost waters divide (Genesis 1) as a middle ground set between them by GOD / so that through this very thing / the Light and uppermost water / can unite with the lowermost physical water / since otherwise the soul without the spirit can never harmonize with the body <note>original: *corpore*</note> / therefore the life-giving impressions <note>original: *Impressiones*</note> of the Light and the uppermost water first moved into the middle waters / which are of a physical and spiritual nature / wherein a spiritual body <note>original: *Spirituale corpus*</note> is immersed / which finally into the lowest water of its kind and quality—

### Cabala Chymica. ###

# The Opened Cabinet of Nature #

Therefore the <term>Philosopher [Philosophus <note>In this context, the "Philosopher" refers to the alchemist or practitioner of the "Sacred Art" of chemistry.</note>]</term> must also divide his whole work into four parts / as the main pillars of his artificial structure / namely into the Light / the upper, middle, and lower Water / and divide or <term>separate [separiren]</term> these from one another.

The Light is the <term>Form [Forma <note>The "Form" is the spiritual or organizing principle that gives a substance its specific identity and qualities.</note>]</term>, the living essence and actual powers / and the burning brilliance of the Soul / or the super-heavenly, incomprehensible fire.

The upper water, however, is the <term>Matter [Materia]</term> or the <term>airy body [Aërisch corpus]</term> of the Soul / its seat and housing / or the imperceptible air / through the joining and influence of both of which a shining / clear / crystalline / super-heavenly <term>Essence [Essentia]</term>, that is / the <term>Soul [Anima]</term>, is formed.

The middle water is to be considered a Form compared to the lower, but a Matter compared to the upper / which is the <term>Spirit [Spiritus]</term>, which is the body of the Soul: the living power, however / Form and essence of the lower <term>corporeal [Corporalischẽ]</term> water / through which the same must be forced / purified and made spiritual / in such a way / that the Spirit is a living water and the true <term>Water of Life [Aqua vitæ <note>original: "Aqua vitæ." While often used for brandy or alcohol today, in alchemy it refers to the universal solvent or life-giving spirit of the work.</note>]</term> / in which the upper Light settles with its crystalline water / by means of which the <term>Body [Corpus]</term>, that is the lower water, is subsequently <term>illuminated [illuminirt]</term> and <term>clarified [clarificirt <note>To "clarify" in alchemy is to purify a substance until it is transparent or "clear" of its worldly dross.</note>]</term> and its previously suppressed / as if deceased life is now for the first time truly awakened and [strengthened]...

### Cabala Chymica ###

...of constant power and magnificence is crowned and clarified.

The lowest water is the **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> and the true **matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note>, in which all the uppermost powers settle according to their given measure; therefore it is a **center** <note>original: *centrum*</note> upon which the **Form** <note>The organizing spiritual principle discussed on the previous page.</note> keeps its eyes and which it desires, and into which the invisible outflows of all the upper waters pour—as if into a lake—and in which they are also made constant and remain fixed.

But because the lowest elemental waters, on account of the committed **Adamic sin** <note>The author links the biblical Fall of Man to a physical corruption of the natural world, suggesting that all matter became "unclean" or "mixed" after Adam's exile from Eden.</note>, had to leave their first state and have fallen from purity into the greatest impurity, all **lower mixed bodies** <note>original: *inferiora corpora mixta*</note> along with their **souls** <note>original: *Animabus*</note> and **spirits** <note>original: *Spiritibus*</note> have been covered and spotted unto their innermost part with the curse of impurity. 

If these three parts—the **Soul** <note>original: *Anima*</note>, the **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note>, and the **body** <note>original: *corpus*</note>—are now to be released from this impurity again and return to their first, pure, and powerful state, they must be broken down and divided from one another, separated several times, and **putrefied** <note>original: *putrificirt*. In alchemy, putrefaction is a process of decomposition used to separate the pure from the impure before a substance can be "reborn."</note> of their **impurities** <note>original: *impura*</note>. Afterward, each part must be brought back to its proper state individually, and finally united and made equal to their first essence—indeed, even more magnificent than it was before. This is what the Holy Moses calls "cultivating and keeping **Paradise**." <note>A reference to Genesis 2:15, interpreted here as an alchemical command to restore nature to its original perfection.</note>

<summary>This page defines the "lowest water" as the physical body of matter and explains that because of the Fall of Adam, all physical substances are corrupted and must undergo alchemical separation and purification to be restored to their original, paradisiacal perfection.</summary>

# Preface to the favorable READER.

Since in this last age of the world and these sorrowful, heavily burdened times, it is found—alas!—in practice and in deed, how strictly and poorly this most precious and divine gift <note>original: *Donum hoc Divinum & pretiosissimum*</note> is despised and suppressed by the enemy of truth through his instruments with hatred, envy, and mockery; that is, the most truthful universal <term>spargiric</term> <note>Spargiric medicine refers to the alchemical process of separating, purifying, and recombining plant or mineral components to create potent remedies, typically associated with Paracelsus.</note> medicine, of the adept art and philosophy, in which alone resides and is to be found that great composition <note>original: *magnum illud compositum*</note> of the upper and lower spheres, their power and capability in simplicity <note>original: *simplicitate*</note>. It also encloses and encompasses the diverse qualities of all elements under the Cabalistic number of one <note>original: *sub unario numero Cabalistico*. This refers to alchemical unity or the *Monas*, where all elements are brought back to a single, perfect state.</note>. Furthermore, in all illnesses and cures <note>original: *& curis*</note>, it maintains victory and preeminence <note>original: *victoriam & principatum*</note> over all other medications, to the honor and praise of God who created them. From this alone, the right, pure, most precious, and most truthful purple potable gold <note>original: *Aurum potabile purpuratum*. This was considered the highest form of medicinal gold, signifying the completion of the "Great Work."</note> of the new birth and regenerated being—which was called **Ambrosia and Nectar** by the ancients—has its origin and is possible to obtain. This is for the benefit of the beloved and far-famed fatherland, in which formerly <note>original: *olim*</note> the <term>adept philosophy</term> <note>The secret knowledge possessed by "adepts," or those who have successfully achieved the goals of alchemy.</note>, by which the secrets of nature are explored <note>original: *quâ naturæ secreta rimantur*</note>, flourished greatly, and now through divine

<meta>catchword: divine [Göttliche]</meta>

<summary>This preface introduces the "universal spargiric medicine" as a divine gift currently under attack by enemies of truth, identifying it as the source of the legendary "potable gold." The author laments the decline of alchemical philosophy in his homeland and frames the work as a restoration of this ancient wisdom.</summary>

<header>Preface</header>

<sig>E</sig>

[...divine] providence <note>original: *Schickung*</note>, and by the scrutinizers of divine and human things <note>original: *& per divinarum & humanarum rerum perscrutatores*</note>, the same was sought out again from the deep abyss <note>original: *ex profundo abyssi*</note> and the deepest philosophical approach <note>original: *& imo Philosoph. aditu*</note>, to be brought to light for the benefit of candidates of true medicine <note>original: *veræ medicinæ candidatis*</note>. Through this, the great distinction and misuse between common potable gold <note>A solution of ordinary gold, often sold as a panacea but considered ineffective by true alchemists.</note> and regenerated philosophical gold <note>original: *auro Philosophico regeneratio*. This refers to gold that has been "killed" and "reborn" through alchemical processes to unlock its medicinal power.</note>—about which it is permitted to thoroughly and faithfully reveal much from the hidden mysteries of nature <note>original: *ex abditis naturæ mysteriis*</note>—may be well understood and recognized.

In this period, "idiot" referred to a layman or someone without formal philosophical training.</note>.

# The First Chapter.

It is found in **adept philosophy and nature's treasure** <note>original: *Philosophia adepta & naturæ thesauro*. "Adept philosophy" refers to the secret knowledge of successful alchemists.</note>, that there are three types of gold **in the nature of things** <note>original: *in rerum natura*</note>. The first is Astral; the second, Elemental; and the third, Metallic. **Paracelsus**, the highly renowned, agrees with this sentiment in his **Book of Vexations** <note>original: *Libello vexationis*. Paracelsus (1493–1541) was a Swiss physician and alchemist who pioneered the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine.</note>, saying that there is similarly **a threefold gold** <note>original: *triplex esse Aurum*</note>: Firmamental <note>Relating to the stars or the heavens.</note>, Elemental, and Metallic. The **Golden Fleece** <note>original: *Aureum vellus*. A famous alchemical compendium first published in 1598.</note> says that **the substance of gold is threefold: simple, compound, and doubly compound** <note>original: *triplicem esse substantiam auri, simplicem, compositam & decompositam*</note>, and others besides are **of the same opinion** <note>original: *ejusdem sunt sententiæ*</note>.

Now, however, none among them **specifically** <note>original: *specificè*</note> describes or reports which one in particular—as the best and most useful among the three—should be used or taken to make the **potable gold of physicians** <note>original: *aurum potabile medicorum*. This was a sought-after alchemical "universal medicine" consisting of gold dissolved in a drinkable form.</note> from it. To cancel and remove this scruple and obstacle along with all doubt, before one may proceed **to what lies beyond** <note>original: *ad plus ultra*</note>, it is necessary to know—when one wishes to undertake the process of **preparation**—that one first becomes well-informed and learns **under what matter and form** <note>original: *sub qua materia & forma*</note> the gold must be shaped and created in its metallic essence, before one wishes to make it medicinal as a drink. Thus it is found, according to proven experience and the opinion of all philosophers, that no **potable medicine from gold** <note>original: *potabile Medicamentum ex auro*</note>

## The Third Chapter.

It is held by all **Philosophers** that the **solution of gold** <note>original: *solutio auri*. Balancing them is a central challenge of the 'Great Work.'</note>. This is something that no one will ever learn through reading or the **study of philosophers** <note>original: *vel Philosophorum studio*</note> alone, unless he has discovered it himself through **manual operation** <note>original: *Manual operation*. This emphasizes practical laboratory work over mere theory.</note> and a hidden faith with **manifold experience** <note>original: *multiplici experientia*</note>. This must be done in such a way that if the **solvent** <note>original: *solvens*. The substance used to dissolve the gold.</note>—the **philosophical potential key** <note>original: *clavis potentialis*</note>—is to fruitfully perform its office, the **active agent** <note>original: *agens*</note> and the **passive subject** <note>original: *patiens*</note> in their penetrating and tingeing...

## The Fourth Chapter.

Because **mention** <note>original: *Mention*</note> was made in the previous chapter of the materials through which the true philosophical **solution** <note>original: *Solutio*; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state.</note> is possible, a more extensive report shall now follow regarding what materials such a **corrosive** <note>original: *Corrosiff*; in alchemy, this refers to a powerful solvent or "water" capable of dissolving metals.</note> ought to be made from, as current **practitioners** <note>original: *Laboranten*</note> go far astray and **err** <note>original: *abusiren*</note> in this matter.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions regarding the "philosophical solution." The previous page established that the "corrosive" solvent must be carefully chosen. This page explains the alchemical principle of sympathy—that substances must be dissolved by materials of their own kind—and introduces the concept of "radical moisture" necessary for extracting the medicinal essence of gold.</meta>

From this, it is to be understood that every thing associates, rejoices, and unites with that which is related to its own nature and quality, in that it willingly opens and closes itself, allowing itself to be internally **anatomized** and disassembled through all things. In this way also, and in no other, it gives off all its **flowers, colors, and odors, virtues, and essences** <note>original: *flores, colores & odores, virtutes & essentias*; the distinct qualities and medicinal powers of a substance.</note>. Therefore, let us be exhorted and warned through this **example and parable** <note>original: *Exempel & parabolam*</note> regarding this **solution of potable gold** <note>original: *auri potabilis solution*; a drinkable medicinal form of gold</note>: that no **species** of **vegetable matter** <note>original: *Vegetabilien*</note> can be **dissolved** <note>original: *resolviren*</note> with **minerals** <note>original: *Mineralien*</note>, nor minerals with vegetable species. Instead, the **metals and minerals** <note>original: *metalla & mineralia*</note> must **immediately** <note>original: *immediatè*</note> be broken down and dissolved again through metallic or mineral species of their own kind.

For how many years has this or that person had to remain outside the fence in this matter, and could not cross over, because the **radical moisture** <note>original: *humidum radicale*; the innate, vital moisture that sustains the life and form of a substance</note> remained unknown to him? Without this, it is impossible to **extract** the **mysteries and secrets** <note>original: *mysteria & arcana*</note> together with the **transparent quintessence of gold** <note>original: *Diaphanischen quinto esse auri*; the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual extract of the metal</note>, or to obtain any **medicinal** remedy from it.

For **like rejoices in like** <note>original: *simile simili gaudet*; the fundamental alchemical principle that substances only interact with their own kind.</note>. Therefore, in this matter, remind yourselves of **philosophical reasons** <note>original: *Philosophischen rationibus*</note> and ruminate on the proven sayings of the philosophers, who say that no water or **corrosive** <note>original: *Corrosiff*; a powerful solvent</note> **dissolves** <note>original: *dissolviret*</note> the species of metals, except for that which [is] with the **matter** <note>original: *materiâ*</note>...

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the "philosophical solution" of metals. ...and form, in **species and form** <note>original: *in specie & forma*</note>, which remains permanent in every examination according to its external and internal essence.

**Lullius** <note>Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1315), a Majorcan philosopher and polymath often associated with medieval alchemy.</note> confirms this in his *Testament*, folio 95: "That every dissolved metal necessarily requires a lodging that is of its own proper matter, in which it may be born and multiplied." **Rupicissa** <note>Jean de Roquetaillade (c. 1310–1366), a Franciscan friar and alchemist known for his work on the quintessence.</note> says: "That no water dissolves metals by true **reduction** <note>The process of returning a metal to its primary, fluidic state.</note> except that which remains permanent in **congelations** <note>original: *congelationibus*; the process of thickening or solidifying a substance.</note>." **Albertus** <note>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280), a prominent Dominican scholar whose name was frequently attached to alchemical treatises.</note> similarly mentions this in his treatise, chapter 8: "That before all things, one must labor so that waters are made which contain within themselves the various qualities of the Elements in equal power, through which they may exalt and **coagulate** <note>To thicken a liquid into a solid or semi-solid state.</note> that which they wish to **transmute** <note>original: *transmutare*; the alchemical process of changing one substance into another.</note>, and by which the dissolved metals themselves can be solidified."

**Augurellus** <note>Giovanni Aurelio Augurello (c. 1441–1524), author of the alchemical poem *Chrysopoeia*.</note>, in Book 1, says: Dissolve gold in its own water; with this water, the ancients of old brought the ship **into the harbor** <note>original: *in portum*; a metaphorical expression for successfully completing the alchemical Great Work.</note>. A Cabalistic author and philosopher writes: that there is only one thing in the whole world that dissolves gold and silver. **Paracelsus** <note>The famous Swiss-German physician and alchemist (1493–1541) who pioneered the use of minerals in medicine.</note> in *On Long Life*, regarding the **Elixir** <note>original: *de Elixere*; a preparation meant to prolong life or transmute metals.</note>: "Resolve gold together with the **corrosive substance** <note>original: *substantia auri corrosivo*; here meaning a solvent that shares the same nature as gold.</note> of gold, and do so until it becomes one and the same with the corrosive."

Others write that a special moisture is to be sought in minerals through miraculous manipulations, which dissolves gold without force. **Walburg, Augurellus, Isaac Hollandus** <note>Johannes Isaac Hollandus and his son were influential 15th-century Dutch alchemists.</note>

### **The Composition of the Universal Tincture to be used**

Regarding this, the greatest philosophers <note>original: *summi Philosophi*</note> say in general <note>original: *in genere*</note> that it is impossible and unbelievable to extract the fifth essence <note>original: *quintam essentiam*; the quintessence, or the purest, most medicinal part of a substance</note> of gold from the sun <note>original: *ex sole*; alchemists used the sun as the symbol for gold</note> without a corrosive agent <note>original: *Corrosiff*; a sharp solvent used to break down metals</note>. For without a corrosive <note>original: *absque corrosivo*</note>, gold <note>original: *aurum*</note> cannot be mastered, as Paracelsus <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a seminal figure in Swiss-German medicine and alchemy.</note> mentions more extensively in *Archidoxis* <note>one of Paracelsus's primary texts on the fundamental principles of alchemy and medicine.</note>, and in his book *On the Long Life* <note>original: *Libro de vita longa*</note>, he may well say that the corrosive agent is greater than the gold itself.

However, as to how this corrosive becomes the highest sweetness in such an operation of potable gold <note>original: *auri potabilis*; "drinkable gold," a legendary alchemical medicine believed to cure all ailments and grant longevity.</note> without any separation of the solvent from the solution <note>original: *solventis à solutio*</note>, and how the potable gold becomes fluid—here Plato bids us to be silent <note>original: *jubet hic Plato quiescere*; a traditional alchemical phrase used to signal that the author is withholding a secret from the uninitiated</note>. Because I have already revealed more than enough in the previous sections—which contain and encompass the warnings, instructions, and proof for attaining true potable gold—I will nevertheless give this most faithful farewell <note>original: *Valete*</note> as something particularly worthy of note <note>original: *notatu dignum*</note> to the pious natural-artists <note>seekers who study the secrets of nature through alchemy.</note> who have not yet drunk from the cup of ruin. I want the practiced artist, in pure humility <note>original: *in pura humilitate*</note>, acting as a skilled laboratory worker <note>original: *Laborant*</note>, to well imagine and strive toward how the firm and strongest castle of the essence of the four elements—along with its highest inhabitant—may be besieged, contested, overcome, and captured, so that in the fourth or fifth storming or assault, nothing remains to be held back.

But the <term>lovers of wealth|Philopluti; from the Greek 'philos' (loving) and 'ploutos' (wealth)</term> and <term>lovers of money|Philocrimati; from the Greek 'philos' (loving) and 'chrimata' (money)</term> have previously, as they do now, wished to turn this <note>original: *donum divinum*</note> divine gift <note>original: *in proprium commodum*</note> to their own advantage. Through this, they have brought themselves as well as this gift into great contempt, mockery, and shame (because they did not find it in the proper way, and instead misused it for imposture and deceit). But **GOD**, who is Truth itself, will <note>original: *tandem*</note> at last [reward] the pious, valiant, and helpful physicians, who have labored until their blood turned sour <note>An idiom meaning to work with exhausting, painful effort</note> and have long sought after the simple, ancient Truth,

->The First.<-

Everyone by nature desires to possess great golden and silver treasures, precious stones, and wealth, and to be seen as great and high in the eyes of the world. **God** <note>original: *GOtt*</note> also created all these things so that mankind might use them, be a master over them, and therein recognize, praise, honor, and thank Him for His divine goodness and omnipotence. 

However, everyone wishes to have sought these things only during pleasant days, without the hardship, danger, and labor where God has placed them. They are unwilling to dig deep, search, or find them, because for a long time now the place and the way thither have been unknown and hidden from the **common masses** <note>original: *meisten Hauffen*; literally "the largest heap" or "the great pile," a common 17th-century term for the uninitiated or the general public</note>. ## The Second Response.

I step into the seven circles <note>term: "Circkul" — referring to the seven planetary spheres of traditional cosmology (Moon, Mercury, Venus, Sun, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn)</note> of heaven / grasp the uppermost with my thoughts / and stand with my feet upon the lowermost circle. Where the moonlight <note>original: "Monschein"</note> dazzles my eyes and I stumble or fall / and break a leg / I simply make a crutch <note>original: "Stelzen"; literally "stilts," but here referring to a support for a broken limb</note> / and go slowly / that is a balsam to my internal salt <note>term: "innerlichen Saltz" — in alchemical theory, Salt represents the physical body or the principle of fixity and crystallization</note> / which heals me again / but if I become sweaty / a lovely sweet water comes out of my / pores <note>original Latin: "poris"</note> / like milk and honey / then I burn my crutch / into pure ash / when the ash glows without smoke / the King's well <note>term: "Königs Brunnen" — an alchemical metaphor for a solvent or a purifying bath in which the "King" (representing gold or the spirit) is cleansed or dissolved</note> yields the marigolds <note>term: "Ringelblumen" — the marigold was a symbol of the sun and spiritual turning toward the divine light</note> / and then three fall into the well and create a darkness of the world until the moonlight breaks through again and becomes clear. The night is passed / the sun gives its light / the days of the Lord approach / heaven becomes pure fire and ignites the whole world. All four elements <note>original Latin: "Elementa"</note> melt away and a new heaven and earth are born. In these brief words is the treasure of the <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword indicates the next page begins with "World."</note>

<summary>This page contains an alchemical and mystical allegory describing a journey through the celestial spheres and a process of spiritual healing and purification. It concludes with an apocalyptic vision of the world being renewed through fire and the birth of a new heaven and earth.</summary>
<keywords>alchemical allegory, seven circles, internal salt, King's well, marigolds, four elements, spiritual transformation, apocalypse</keywords>

...perfectly comprehended <note>original: "begriffen"</note> in the world / so that your desire may be fulfilled. Our Brotherhood has also communicated this without any recompense <note>original: "wiedervergelten"; meaning freely or without charge</note>. But present yourselves again on Christmas Day at St. Peter’s <note>Referring to a specific location or a symbolic station within the Order's ritual calendar</note> in our Order; the B. T. <note>These initials likely refer to an alchemical substance or a grade within the order, such as "Benedictum Thesaurum" (Blessed Treasure)</note> shall then also follow you.
